Late term expansion — outer solution

The previous procedure allowed us to approximate the solution when close to singularities. Nevertheless, we note that the ansatz (
34
) is not so accurate away from them as it lacks the information provided by the
expansion of the
parameters,
a
and
b
. For that reason, we need to consider a more general ansatz away from the singularities. Let

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{n,r}=\frac{\kappa^{n-1}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}}}\,\sum_{s\geq 0}\frac{\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}(X)}{n^{s/2}},

(50)

where we have used the summation over natural halved orders to take into account all the terms we obtained in our expansion for the amplitude up to order five (see Section
2
). With this, note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{n-2,r}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\begin{multlined}\frac{1}{2}\,\kappa^{n-3}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-3}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}}}\sum_{s\geq 0}\frac{1}{(n-2)^{s/2}}\left(\vphantom{\left(X_{0}-X\right)\,\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right)^{\prime}}\left(n-3+2\,\gamma_{r}\right)\,\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right.\\
\left.+2\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)\,\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right)^{\prime}\right),\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{n-4,r}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\,\kappa^{n-5}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-5}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}}}\sum_{s\geq 0}\frac{1}{(n-4)^{s/2}}\left(\vphantom{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{2}\,\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right)^{\prime\prime}}\left(n-5+2\,\gamma_{r}\right)\left(n-3+2\,\gamma_{r}\right)\,\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+4\,\left(n-5+2\,\gamma_{r}\right)\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)\,\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right)^{\prime}+4\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{2}\,\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right).

Thus, when replacing (
50
) into (
33
), we obtain

\mathbf{0}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p=0\\
\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{e}=p\end{subarray}}^{n-1}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=0\\
\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{e}=q\end{subarray}}^{n-1-p}\left(C_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}}\,\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}a_{s}^{p_{s}}\right)\,\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}b_{s}^{q_{s}}\right)\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{p,q}(\mathbf{0})\,\kappa^{n-1-p-q}\right.\\
\left.\times\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-p-q-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-p-q-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}}}\,\sum_{s\geq 0}\frac{\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}}{(n-p-q)^{s/2}}\right)\\
+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p=0\\
\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{e}=p\end{subarray}}^{n-1}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=0\\
\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{e}=q\end{subarray}}^{n-1-p}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}M=2\\
\mathbf{n}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{M}=n-p-q\end{subarray}}^{n-p-q}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{r}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{M}=r\\
-n_{i}\leq r_{i}\leq n_{i}\end{subarray}}\left(C_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}}\,\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}a_{s}^{p_{s}}\right)\,\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}b_{s}^{q_{s}}\right)\right.\\
\left.\times\mathbf{F}_{M,p,q}\left(A_{n_{1},r_{1}}\,\mathbf{W}_{n_{1},r_{1}},\ldots,A_{n_{M},r_{M}}\,\mathbf{W}_{n_{M},r_{M}}\right)\vphantom{C_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}}\,\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}a_{s}^{p_{s}}\right)\,\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}b_{s}^{q_{s}}\right)}\right)\\
+k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\left(-r^{2}\,\kappa^{n-1}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}}}\,\sum_{s\geq 0}\frac{\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}}{n^{s/2}}\right.\\
+2\,i\,r\,\begin{multlined}\left(\frac{1}{2}\,\kappa^{n-3}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-3}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}}}\right.\\
\left.\times\sum_{s\geq 0}\frac{1}{(n-2)^{s/2}}\left(\left(n-3+2\,\gamma_{r}\right)\,\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}+2\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)\,\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right)^{\prime}\right)\right)\end{multlined}\\
+\frac{1}{4}\,\kappa^{n-5}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-5}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}}}\sum_{s\geq 0}\frac{1}{(n-4)^{s/2}}\left(\left(n-5+2\,\gamma_{r}\right)\left(n-3+2\,\gamma_{r}\right)\,\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right.\\
\left.\left.+4\,\left(n-5+2\,\gamma_{r}\right)\left(X_{0}-X\right)\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right)^{\prime}+4\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{2}\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\right),

which is equivalent to

\mathbf{0}=\Gamma\left(\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)^{-1}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p=0\\
\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{e}=p\end{subarray}}^{n-1}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=0\\
\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{e}=q\end{subarray}}^{n-1-p}\left(C_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}}\,\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}a_{s}^{p_{s}}\right)\,\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}b_{s}^{q_{s}}\right)\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{p,q}(\mathbf{0})\,\kappa^{4-p-q}\right.\\
\left.\times\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-p-q}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{-p-q}{2}}}\,\sum_{s\geq 0}\frac{\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}}{(n-p-q)^{s/2}}\right)\\
+\kappa^{5-n}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)^{-1}\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p=0\\
\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{e}=p\end{subarray}}^{n-1}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=0\\
\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{e}=q\end{subarray}}^{n-1-p}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}M=2\\
\mathbf{n}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{M}=n-p-q\end{subarray}}^{n-p-q}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{r}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{M}=r\\
-n_{i}\leq r_{i}\leq n_{i}\end{subarray}}\left(\vphantom{\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}a_{s}^{p_{s}}\right)\,\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}b_{s}^{q_{s}}\right)}C_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}}\right.\\
\left.\times\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}a_{s}^{p_{s}}\right)\,\left(\prod_{s\geq 1}b_{s}^{q_{s}}\right)\,\mathbf{F}_{M,p,q}\left(A_{n_{1},r_{1}}\,\mathbf{W}_{n_{1},r_{1}},\ldots,A_{n_{M},r_{M}}\,\mathbf{W}_{n_{M},r_{M}}\right)\right)\\
+k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\left(-r^{2}\,\kappa^{4}\,\sum_{s\geq 0}\frac{\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}}{n^{s/2}}\right.\\
+2\,i\,r\,\kappa^{2}\,\sum_{s\geq 0}\frac{1}{(n-2)^{s/2}}\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}+\frac{2}{n-3+2\,\gamma_{r}}\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)\,\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right)^{\prime}\right)\\
+\sum_{s\geq 0}\frac{1}{(n-4)^{s/2}}\left(\mathbf{v}_{r}^{[s/2]}+\frac{4}{n-3+2\,\gamma_{r}}\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right)^{\prime}\right.\\
\left.\left.+\frac{4}{\left(n-5+2\,\gamma_{r}\right)\left(n-3+2\,\gamma_{r}\right)}\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{2}\left(\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[s/2]}\right)^{\prime\prime}\right)\right),

(51)

Now, as usual, we go forward by following an order-by-order expansion.

4.3
Solving a coupled system of amplitude equations

Note that the pairs of equations (
52
)–(
53
) and (
54
)–(
55
) are symmetric. Thus, it is enough to solve one pair of them in order to know the solutions for both. In particular, we focus on solving
the pair (
52
)–(
53
), which is given by

\alpha_{1}\,\left(h_{0}^{[0]}\right)^{\prime\prime}-i\left(\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\right)\,\left(h_{0}^{[0]}\right)^{\prime}\\
+\left(\alpha_{5}+2\,\alpha_{6}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}+3\,\alpha_{7}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{4}-i\left(\alpha_{3}\,A_{1}\,\bar{A}_{1}^{\prime}+2\,\alpha_{4}\,\bar{A}_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}\right)\right)\,h_{0}^{[0]}\\
-i\,\alpha_{4}\,\bar{A}_{1}^{2}\,\left(h_{2}^{[0]}\right)^{\prime}+\left(\alpha_{6}\,\bar{A}_{1}^{2}+2\,\alpha_{7}\,\bar{A}_{1}^{2}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}-i\,\alpha_{3}\,\bar{A}_{1}\,\bar{A}_{1}^{\prime}\right)\,h_{2}^{[0]}=0,

(56)

and

\alpha_{1}\,\left(h_{2}^{[0]}\right)^{\prime\prime}+i\,\left(\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\right)\,\left(h_{2}^{[0]}\right)^{\prime}\\
+\left(\alpha_{5}+2\,\alpha_{6}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}+3\,\alpha_{7}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{4}+i\,\left(\alpha_{3}\,\bar{A}_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}+2\,\alpha_{4}\,A_{1}\,\bar{A}_{1}^{\prime}\right)\right)\,h_{2}^{[0]}\\
+i\,\alpha_{4}\,A_{1}^{2}\,\left(h_{0}^{[0]}\right)^{\prime}+\left(\alpha_{6}\,A_{1}^{2}+2\,\alpha_{7}\,A_{1}^{2}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}+i\,\alpha_{3}\,A_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}\right)\,h_{0}^{[0]}=0.

(57)

To solve these equations, we note that (
57
) is
a
complex conjugate version of (
56
). Furthermore, based on
[
10
,
13
]
, and by looking at the equations
R_{1}
and

\displaystyle h_{0}^{[0]}=\left(R_{2}-i\,R_{1}\,\varphi_{2}\right)\,e^{-i\,\varphi_{1}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad h_{2}^{[0]}=\left(R_{2}+i\,R_{1}\,\varphi_{2}\right)\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}},

where
R_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=R_{2}(X)}
and
\varphi_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\varphi_{2}(X)}
are real functions. Therefore, when we replace this into (
56
), we obtain

-48\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,\beta_{5}\,R_{1}^{5}\,\varphi_{2}-32\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,\beta_{3}\,R_{1}^{3}\,\varphi_{2}+16\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,\varphi_{2}\,R_{1}^{\prime\prime}+8\,\alpha_{1}\,\alpha_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}\,R_{2}^{\prime}+8\,\alpha_{1}\,\alpha_{4}\,R_{1}^{2}\,R_{2}^{\prime}\\
+32\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\varphi_{2}^{\prime}+24\,\alpha_{1}\,\alpha_{3}\,R_{2}\,R_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}+24\,\alpha_{1}\,\alpha_{4}\,R_{2}\,R_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}-16\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,\beta_{1}\,R_{1}\,\varphi_{2}+16\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,R_{1}\,\varphi_{2}^{\prime\prime}\\
+i\,\left(-240\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,\beta_{5}\,R_{2}\,R_{1}^{4}-4\,\alpha_{3}^{2}\,R_{2}\,R_{1}^{4}+12\,\alpha_{4}^{2}\,R_{2}\,R_{1}^{4}+8\,\alpha_{3}\,\alpha_{4}\,R_{2}\,R_{1}^{4}-8\,\alpha_{1}\,\alpha_{3}\,R_{1}^{3}\,\varphi_{2}^{\prime}\right.\\
\left.+24\,\alpha_{1}\,\alpha_{4}\,R_{1}^{3}\,\phi_{2}^{\prime}-96\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,\beta_{3}\,R_{2}\,R_{1}^{2}-16\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,\beta_{1}\,R_{2}+16\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,R_{2}^{\prime\prime}\right)=0.

Here, as all the coefficients and functions are real, we have that the real and imaginary parts of the previous equation must equal zero to fulfill it. Then, after splitting the equation and simplifying it using (
17
), (
18
), and
\beta_{3}^{2}=4\,\beta_{1}\,\beta_{5}
, we obtain

\displaystyle 4\,\alpha_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\varphi_{2}^{\prime}+3\,\left(\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}\right)\,R_{1}\,R_{2}\,R_{1}^{\prime}+R_{1}\,\left(2\,\alpha_{1}\,\varphi_{2}^{\prime\prime}+\left(\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}\right)\,R_{1}\,R_{2}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=0,

(58)

and

4\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,\left(R_{2}^{\prime\prime}-\beta_{1}\,R_{2}\right)\\
-R_{1}^{2}\,\left(R_{2}\,\left(24\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,\beta_{3}+R_{1}^{2}\,\left(60\,\alpha_{1}^{2}\,\beta_{5}+\left(\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}\right)\,\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\right)\right)\right.\\
\left.+2\,\alpha_{1}\,\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,R_{1}\,\varphi_{2}^{\prime}\right)=0.

(59)

Note that (
58
) can be directly solved for
\varphi_{2}^{\prime}
. Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}^{\prime}=-\frac{\left(\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}\right)\,R_{1}\,R_{2}}{2\,\alpha_{1}}+\frac{4\,\alpha_{1}\,K_{3}}{R_{1}^{2}},

where
K_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
is a constant of integration. Now, when replacing this expression into (
59
), we obtain

\displaystyle R_{2}^{\prime\prime}-\left(\beta_{1}+6\,\beta_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}+15\,\beta_{5}\,R_{1}^{4}\right)\,R_{2}=2\,\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,K_{3}\,R_{1},

(60)

which is a linear non-homogeneous equation in
R_{2}
. To solve it, note that the choice
K_{3}=0
makes the equation homogeneous, with a solution given by

\displaystyle R_{2,h}=R_{1}^{\prime}\,\left(K_{1}+K_{2}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{\differential s}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right).

Now, observe that if
K_{1}=K_{2}=K_{3}=0
, then
\varphi_{2}=K_{4}\in\mathbb{R}
is a constant, which leads to

\displaystyle h_{0}^{[0]}

\displaystyle=-i\,K_{4}\,R_{1}\,e^{-i\,\varphi_{1}}=-i\,K_{4}\,\bar{A}_{1},

\displaystyle h_{2}^{[0]}

\displaystyle=i\,K_{4}\,R_{1}\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}}=i\,K_{4}\,A_{1}.

On the other hand, when
K_{2}=K_{3}=0
, we have that
R_{2,h}=K_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}
, which implies

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}^{\prime}=-K_{1}\,\frac{\left(\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}\right)\,\left(R_{1}^{2}\right)^{\prime}}{4\,\alpha_{1}}=-K_{1}\,\varphi_{1}^{\prime\prime}.

This yields
\varphi_{2}=-K_{1}\,\varphi_{1}^{\prime}
(setting the constant of integration to zero), which implies

\displaystyle h_{0}^{[0]}

\displaystyle=\left(K_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}-i\,K_{1}\,R_{1}\,\varphi_{1}^{\prime}\right)\,e^{-i\,\varphi_{1}}=K_{1}\,\bar{A}_{1}^{\prime},

\displaystyle h_{2}^{[0]}

\displaystyle=\left(K_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}+i\,K_{1}\,R_{1}\,\varphi_{1}^{\prime}\right)\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}}=K_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}.

Now, if
K_{3}\neq 0
, then we can use the method of variation of parameters to find the solution to the non-homogeneous equation, (
60
). In particular, we have

\displaystyle R_{2,p}=\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,K_{3}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\displaystyle{\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s}.

Therefore, we conclude that, when
K_{1}=0
,

\displaystyle R_{2}=R_{1}^{\prime}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{K_{2}+\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,K_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s,

which implies

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}=-\frac{\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}}{2\,\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\left(R_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{s}\frac{K_{2}+\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,K_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential\sigma\right)\,\differential s+4\,\alpha_{1}\,K_{3}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{\differential s}{R_{1}^{2}}.

Thus, when gathering all the information we have obtained, we conclude that

\displaystyle h_{2}^{[0]}

\displaystyle=\begin{multlined}K_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}+i\,K_{4}\,A_{1}+\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{K_{2}+\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,K_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s\right.\\
\left.+i\,R_{1}\,\left(-\frac{\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}}{2\,\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\left(R_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{s}\frac{K_{2}+\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,K_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential\sigma\right)\,\differential s\right.\right.\\
\left.\left.+4\,\alpha_{1}\,K_{3}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{\differential s}{R_{1}^{2}}\right)\right)\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}},\end{multlined}

and

\displaystyle h_{0}^{[0]}

\displaystyle=\begin{multlined}K_{1}\,\bar{A}_{1}^{\prime}-i\,K_{4}\,\bar{A}_{1}+\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{K_{2}+\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,K_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s\right.\\
\left.-i\,R_{1}\,\left(-\frac{\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}}{2\,\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\left(R_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{s}\frac{K_{2}+\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,K_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential\sigma\right)\,\differential s\right.\right.\\
\left.\left.+4\,\alpha_{1}\,K_{3}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{\differential s}{R_{1}^{2}}\right)\right)\,e^{-i\,\varphi_{1}}.\end{multlined}

Remark 3
.

We highlight that we solved the amplitude equations in this section by assuming that
R_{2}
and
\varphi_{2}
were real functions. Nevertheless, this is not the case when making an integration involving
X_{0}
in the limits of integration. In reality, we are only interested in the case where
X
is real. However, as we have been focusing on the study of singularities, we need to centre our solutions on them in every step to
be consistent with
our analysis.

4.4
Matching inner and outer solutions

We have obtained an inner and an outer solution for our late-term expansion. To match these, we need

\displaystyle\kappa^{n-1}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)\,\frac{\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[0]}(X)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}}}\sim\kappa^{n-1}\,\Gamma\left(\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma_{r}\right)\,\frac{\mathbf{v}_{r}^{[0]}}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n}{2}-r\,\eta\,i}},

which implies

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}_{r}^{[0]}(X)\sim\frac{\mathbf{v}_{r}^{[0]}}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-r\,\eta\,i-\gamma_{r}}}.

In particular, we note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}_{-2}^{[0]}(X)\sim\frac{\mathbf{v}_{-2}^{[0]}}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{1}{2}+2\,\eta\,i-\gamma}},\quad\mathbf{g}_{0}^{[0]}(X)\sim\frac{\mathbf{v}_{0}^{[0]}}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}},\quad\mathbf{g}_{2}^{[0]}(X)\sim\frac{\mathbf{v}_{2}^{[0]}}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-2\,\eta\,i-\gamma}}.

Furthermore, when
\gamma=3-\eta\,i
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}_{-2}^{[0]}(X)\sim\frac{\mathbf{v}_{-2}^{[0]}}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{-\frac{5}{2}+3\,\eta\,i}},\qquad\mathbf{g}_{0}^{[0]}(X)\sim\frac{\mathbf{v}_{0}^{[0]}}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{-\frac{5}{2}+\eta\,i}},\qquad\mathbf{g}_{2}^{[0]}(X)\sim\frac{\mathbf{v}_{2}^{[0]}}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{-\frac{5}{2}-\eta\,i}}.

Now, we observe that

\displaystyle h_{2}^{[0]}\sim\begin{multlined}K_{1}\,\left(\frac{i}{2}\,(2\,\eta+i)\,e^{\pi\,\xi}\,\left(2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\right)^{1/2}\,\left(-2\,\beta_{3}\right)^{-1/2}\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\eta\,i}\,\left(\frac{-1}{X_{0}-X}\right)^{3/2}\right)\\
+K_{2}\,\left(-\frac{1}{6}\,\frac{(3+2\,\eta\,i)\,e^{\pi\,\xi}\,\left(2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\right)^{-1/2}\,\left(-2\,\beta_{3}\right)^{1/2}\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{2+\eta\,i}}{\sqrt{\frac{-1}{X_{0}-X}}}\right)\\
+K_{3}\,\left(\frac{i\,\left(\alpha_{3}\,(3+4\,\eta\,(\eta-i))-3\,\alpha_{4}\,(2\,\eta-i)^{2}\right)\,e^{\pi\,\xi}\,\left(2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\right)^{1/2}\,\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{1+\eta\,i}}{6\,\eta\,\left(-2\,\beta_{3}\right)^{1/2}\,\sqrt{\frac{-1}{X_{0}-X}}}\right)\\
+K_{4}\,\left(i\,e^{\pi\,\xi}\,\left(2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\right)^{1/2}\,\left(-2\,\beta_{3}\right)^{-1/2}\,\sqrt{\frac{-1}{X_{0}-X}}\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\eta\,i}\right),\end{multlined}

which implies that the analysis done previously for the inner solution is matched only by the dominant term associated with
K_{2}
, which lets us conclude that

\displaystyle h_{2}^{[0]}\sim K_{2}\,\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{\differential s}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}-\frac{\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}}{2\,\alpha_{1}}\,i\,R_{1}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\left(R_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{s}\frac{\differential\sigma}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right)\,\differential s\right)\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}}.

Furthermore, by matching this solution with (
49
) as
X\to X_{0}
, we obtain

\displaystyle K_{2}=-\frac{6\,i\,e^{-\pi\,\xi}\,\left(2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\right)^{1/2}\,\left(-2\,\beta_{3}\right)^{-1/2}}{(3+2\,\eta\,i)}\,\lambda_{1}.

(61)

Moreover
, we conclude that the contribution to the solution provided by
X_{0}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}^{[n]}

\displaystyle\sim i^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\gamma}}\,\left(1+(-1)^{n}\right)\,\left(h_{0}^{[0]}+h_{2}^{[0]}\,e^{2ix}\right)\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}

(62)

\displaystyle+(-i)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-1}{2}+\bar{\gamma}\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\bar{\gamma}}}\,\left(1+(-1)^{n}\right)\,\left(\overline{h_{0}^{[0]}}+\overline{h_{-2}^{[0]}}\,e^{-2ix}\right)\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]},

as
n\to\infty
. Similarly, due to symmetry, we have that the contribution to the solution provided by
\bar{X}_{0}=X_{-1}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}^{[n]}

\displaystyle\sim(-i)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-1}{2}+\bar{\gamma}\right)}{\left(\bar{X}_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\bar{\gamma}}}\,\left(1+(-1)^{n}\right)\,\left(\overline{h_{0}^{[0]}}+\overline{h_{2}^{[0]}}\,e^{-2ix}\right)\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}

\displaystyle\quad+i^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{n-1}{2}+\bar{\gamma}\right)}{\left(\bar{X}_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{n-1}{2}+\bar{\gamma}}}\,\left(1+(-1)^{n}\right)\,\left(h_{0}^{[0]}+h_{-2}^{[0]}\,e^{2ix}\right)\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]},

as
n\to\infty
.

Remark 4
.

We highlight that the last few expressions correspond to an abuse of notation. Let us recall that
R_{2}
and
\varphi_{2}
should be real functions (see Remark
3
), which would make
h_{0}^{[2]}=\overline{h_{2}^{[0]}}
. Nevertheless, as we are working at complex singularities, this is no longer the case. One technically needs to be extra careful with the terms that are being complex-conjugated and consider the fact that there are more constants of integration fulfilling different rules that can be deduced in the same way as when we matched solutions in this section. In any case, the development shown here is enough for our purposes, and it is consistent with
[
10
,
13
]
.

5
Estimation of the residual

In this section, we start the study of the residual that arises after truncating the asymptotic expansion we have been carrying out. Specifically, if we assume that we have performed the expansion up to a sufficiently large order, then the remainder is expected to be small. Therefore, we are only interested in studying its linearized equation, which turns out to approximate it well and will let us obtain more conditions to ensure that such a term tends to zero as
X\to\pm\infty
.

We begin by introducing new notation for the linearized equation of the residual. In particular, we define the following symmetric multilinear vector functions in terms of the Jacobian of (
4
):

\displaystyle J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle=\left.\left(\sum_{p_{1}=1}^{n}v_{1,p_{1}}\frac{\partial J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{i,j}(\mathbf{u})}{\partial u_{p_{1}}}\right)\right|_{\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{0}},

\displaystyle J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{2,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2!}\,\left.\left(\sum_{p_{1},p_{2}=1}^{n}v_{1,p_{1}}\,v_{2,p_{2}}\,\frac{\partial^{2}J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{i,j}(\mathbf{u})}{\partial u_{p_{1}}\partial u_{p_{2}}}\right)\right|_{\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{0}},

\displaystyle J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{3,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{v}_{3}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3!}\,\left.\left(\sum_{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3}=1}^{n}v_{1,p_{1}}\,v_{2,p_{2}}\,v_{3,p_{3}}\,\frac{\partial^{3}J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{i,j}(\mathbf{u})}{\partial u_{p_{1}}\partial u_{p_{2}}\partial u_{p_{3}}}\right)\right|_{\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{0}},

\displaystyle J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{4,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{v}_{3},\mathbf{v}_{4}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4!}\,\left.\left(\sum_{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4}=1}^{n}v_{1,p_{1}}\,v_{2,p_{2}}\,v_{3,p_{3}}\,v_{4,p_{4}}\,\frac{\partial^{4}J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{i,j}(\mathbf{u})}{\partial u_{p_{1}}\partial u_{p_{2}}\partial u_{p_{3}}\partial u_{p_{4}}}\right)\right|_{\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{0}}.

However, although using these functions is useful for stating the equations for the remainder at each order, they will not let us spot key relationships between the vectors that define the remainder and the vectors obtained in Section
2
. To spot these relationships, we consider the following result:

Lemma 3
.

For every pair of non-negative integers
i,j
, and every vectors
\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{v}_{3},\mathbf{v}_{4},\mathbf{v}_{5}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)\,\mathbf{v}_{2}

\displaystyle=2\,\mathbf{F}_{2,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}\right),

\displaystyle J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{2,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}\right)\,\mathbf{v}_{3}

\displaystyle=3\,\mathbf{F}_{3,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{v}_{3}\right),

\displaystyle J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{3,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{v}_{3}\right)\,\mathbf{v}_{4}

\displaystyle=4\,\mathbf{F}_{4,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{v}_{3},\mathbf{v}_{4}\right),

\displaystyle J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{4,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{v}_{3},\mathbf{v}_{4}\right)\,\mathbf{v}_{5}

\displaystyle=5\,\mathbf{F}_{5,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2},\mathbf{v}_{3},\mathbf{v}_{4},\mathbf{v}_{5}\right).

Proof.

The proof is similar for each equality. Therefore, for simplicity, we give explicit proof of the first equality only. Observe that

\displaystyle J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1}\right)\,\mathbf{v}_{2}

\displaystyle=\left.\left(\sum_{p=1}^{n}v_{1,p}\,\frac{\partial J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{i,j}(\mathbf{u})}{\partial u_{p}}\right)\right|_{\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{0}}\,\mathbf{v}_{2}

\displaystyle=\left.\left(\sum_{p=1}^{n}\sum_{q=1}^{n}v_{1,p}\,v_{2,q}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{f}_{i,j}(\mathbf{u})}{\partial u_{q}\partial u_{p}}\right)\right|_{\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{0}}=2\,\mathbf{F}_{2,i,j}\left(\mathbf{v}_{1},\mathbf{v}_{2}\right),

which proves the first equality and, therefore, as explained above, the result.
∎

The next step is to estimate the remainder
due to the truncation of the asymptotic expansion
we are carrying out. To do that, we consider that our solution can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{S}+\mathbf{R}_{N},

where
\mathbf{S}=\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{N}\varepsilon^{n}\,\mathbf{u}^{[n]}}
is the approximation of the solution we have developed so far, and
\mathbf{R}_{N}
is the remainder, which is assumed to be small. We can therefore linearize (
4
) about
\mathbf{S}
, considering that
\mathbf{S}
solves the equation up to order
N
, obtaining

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=0}^{M_{a}}\,\sum_{q=0}^{M_{b}}a^{p}\,b^{q}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{p,q}(\mathbf{S})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}+k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N}}{\partial x^{2}}+2\,\varepsilon^{2}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N}}{\partial x\partial X}+\varepsilon^{4}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N}}{\partial X^{2}}\right)+\mathbf{E}(\delta b)

(63)

\displaystyle+\text{forcing due to truncation},

where ‘forcing due to truncation’ corresponds to terms of order
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{N+1}\right)
that come out of the evaluation of
\mathbf{S}
in the linear operator due to the truncation of the expansion, and
\mathbf{E}(\delta b)
corresponds to the term that appears when
b
gets away from the Maxwell point. Now, as usual, we need to solve (
63
) going order by order by expanding
\mathbf{R}_{N}
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{N}=\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{p}\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[p]}.

Fortunately, the corresponding solutions at each order can be written in terms of the solutions we already obtained in Section
2
, as
it can be seen
below.

Order
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)
:

At this order, (
63
) becomes

\displaystyle\left(J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,0}(\mathbf{0})+k^{2}\,\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}\right)\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}=\mathbf{0},

which has a solution

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}=B_{1}\,e^{ix}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}+c.c.,

where
B_{1}=B_{1}(X)
.

Order
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)
:

At this order, (
63
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}=J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[2]}+a_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{1,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}+b_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,1}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}+J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,0,0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}+k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[2]}}{\partial x^{2}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[2]}=2\,A_{1}\,\bar{B}_{1}\,\mathbf{W}_{0}^{[2]}+B_{1}\,e^{ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{1}^{[2]}+2\,A_{1}\,B_{1}\,e^{2ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{2}^{[2]}+B_{2}\,e^{ix}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}+c.c.,

where
B_{2}=B_{2}(X)
.

Order
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{3}\right)
:

At this order, (
63
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle=J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[3]}+a_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{1,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[2]}+b_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,1}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[2]}+a_{2}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{1,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}+b_{2}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,1}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}

\displaystyle\quad+a_{1}^{2}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{2,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}+a_{1}\,b_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{1,1}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}+b_{1}^{2}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,2}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}+J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,0,0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[2]}

\displaystyle\quad+J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,0,0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[2]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}+a_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,1,0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}+b_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,0,1}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}

\displaystyle\quad+J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{2,0,0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]},\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}+k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[3]}}{\partial x^{2}}+2\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}}{\partial x\partial X}\right),

which implies that

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[3]}

\displaystyle=2\,A_{1}\,\bar{B}_{1}\,\mathbf{W}_{0}^{[3]}+B_{1}\,e^{ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{1}^{[3]}+A_{1}^{2}\,\bar{B}_{1}\,e^{ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{1,2}^{[3]}+2\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,B_{1}\,e^{ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{1,2}^{[3]}+i\,B_{1}^{\prime}\,e^{ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{1,3}^{[3]}

\displaystyle+2\,A_{1}\,B_{1}\,e^{2ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{2}^{[3]}+2\,A_{2}\,B_{1}\,e^{2ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{2}^{[2]}+3\,A_{1}^{2}\,B_{1}\,e^{3ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{3}^{[3]}+2\,A_{1}\,\bar{B}_{2}\,\mathbf{W}_{0}^{[2]}+2\,A_{2}\,\bar{B}_{1}\,\mathbf{W}_{0}^{[2]}

\displaystyle\quad+B_{2}\,e^{ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{1}^{[2]}+B_{3}\,e^{ix}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}+2\,A_{1}\,B_{2}\,e^{2ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{2}^{[2]}+c.c.,

where
B_{3}=B_{3}(X)
.

Order
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{4}\right)
:

At this order, (
63
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle=J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[4]}+a_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{1,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[3]}+b_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,1}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[3]}+a_{2}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{1,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[2]}

\displaystyle\alpha_{5}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=}-\alpha_{1}\,\beta_{1}-\frac{\alpha_{2}^{2}}{4\,\alpha_{1}},

\displaystyle\alpha_{6}

\displaystyle=-2\,\alpha_{1}\,\beta_{3}-\frac{\alpha_{2}\,\left(\alpha_{3}-\alpha_{4}\right)}{2\,\alpha_{1}},

\displaystyle\alpha_{7}

\displaystyle=-3\,\alpha_{1}\,\beta_{5}-\frac{\left(\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}\right)\,\left(3\,\alpha_{3}-5\,\alpha_{4}\right)}{16\,\alpha_{1}}

With this, (
68
) becomes

\displaystyle R_{1}^{2}\,\varphi_{B}^{\prime\prime}+2\,R_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\varphi_{B}^{\prime}=-\frac{\left(\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}\right)}{2\,\alpha_{1}}\left(R_{1}^{3}\,R_{B}\right)^{\prime},

which implies

\displaystyle\varphi_{B}^{\prime}=-\frac{\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}}{2\,\alpha_{1}}\,R_{1}\,R_{B}+4\,\alpha_{1}\,\frac{L_{3}}{R_{1}^{2}}.

(69)

Therefore, when replacing (
69
) into (
67
), we obtain

\displaystyle R_{B}^{\prime\prime}-\left(\beta_{1}+6\,\beta_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}+15\,\beta_{5}\,R_{1}^{4}\right)\,R_{B}=2\,\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,L_{3}\,R_{1}-\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\,\frac{\differential\delta}{\differential R_{1}}\left(R_{1}\right)=0,

(70)

which is a non-homogeneous second-order equation just as (
60
), but with an extra non-homogeneous term. The solutions will then have the same form
in addition to
an extra term. In particular, the homogeneous solution to (
70
) is given by

\displaystyle R_{B,h}(X)=R_{1}^{\prime}\,\left(L_{1}+L_{2}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{\differential s}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right),

whilst the particular one is given by

\displaystyle R_{B,p}(X)=R_{1}^{\prime}\,\left(\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,L_{3}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s-\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{\delta\left(R_{1}\right)}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s\right).

Therefore, by excluding
L_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle R_{B}(X)=R_{1}^{\prime}\,\left(\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{L_{2}+\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,L_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s-\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{\delta\left(R_{1}\right)}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s\right),

which implies

\displaystyle\varphi_{B}(X)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}}{2\,\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}R_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\left(\int_{X_{0}}^{\omega}\frac{L_{2}+\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,L_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s-\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{\omega}\frac{\delta\left(R_{1}\right)}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s\right)\,\differential\omega

\displaystyle\quad+4\,\alpha_{1}\,L_{3}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{1}{R_{1}^{2}}\,\differential s,

and

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=\left(R_{B}+i\,R_{1}\,\varphi_{B}\right)\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}}

\displaystyle=\begin{multlined}L_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}-i\,L_{4}\,\bar{A}_{1}+\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\,\left(\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{L_{2}+\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,L_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s-\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{\delta\left(R_{1}\right)}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s\right)\right.\\
+i\,R_{1}\,\left(4\,\alpha_{1}\,L_{3}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{1}{R_{1}^{2}}\,\differential s\right.\\
-\frac{\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}}{2\,\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}R_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\left(\int_{X_{0}}^{\omega}\frac{L_{2}+\left(\alpha_{3}-3\,\alpha_{4}\right)\,L_{3}\,R_{1}^{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s\right.\\
\left.\left.\left.-\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{\omega}\frac{\delta\left(R_{1}\right)}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s\right)\,\differential\omega\right)\right)\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}(X)},\end{multlined}

Remark 5
.

Once again,
R_{B}
and
\varphi_{B}
must, technically, be real functions (see Remark
3
). Nevertheless, we make the integration centred at
X_{0}
to continue our analysis at singularities.

6
Remainder with forcing due to truncation

In Section
5
, we analysed the equation for the remainder whilst ignoring the forcing due to truncation. That helped us obtain a general expression for the amplitude of the residual, but the solution we obtained for it misses a term. In particular, we note that, due to the linearity of equation (
63
), some constants of integration need to be determined in order to know the actual expression of
the
remainder. To find such constants, we focus our attention on a Stokes’ line, at which key dominant terms get
triggered
,
letting
us focus on the key part of the remainder to study its boundedness.
Specifically,
with the forcing due to truncation, equation (
63
) becomes:

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=0}^{M_{a}}\sum_{q=1}^{M_{b}}a^{p}\,b^{q}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{p,q}(\mathbf{S})\,\mathbf{R}_{N}+k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N}}{\partial x^{2}}+2\,\varepsilon^{2}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N}}{\partial x\partial X}+\varepsilon^{4}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N}}{\partial X^{2}}\right)

(75)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{N+1}\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\left(2\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{u}^{[N-1]}}{\partial x\partial X}+\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{u}^{[N-3]}}{\partial X^{2}}\right)+\varepsilon^{N+3}\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{u}^{[N-1]}}{\partial X^{2}}.

Also, l
et us recall that our leading-order solution provided by
X_{0}
is given by (
62
). Therefore, the dominant terms triggered by
X_{0}
, present in the forcing of (
75
), are given by

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{N+1}\,\kappa^{N}\,e^{i\hat{x}}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}\sum_{r=0,2}e^{i(r-1)\hat{x}}\,\mathbf{C}_{r},

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{r}

\displaystyle=h_{r}^{[0]}\,\left(\frac{1+2\,i\,\kappa^{2}\,r}{\kappa^{4}}-\varepsilon^{2}\,\left(\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\right)\,\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}\,\left(X-X_{0}\right)}\right)\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}.

Furthermore, two consecutive terms have a ratio given, approximately, by

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{N+3}\,\kappa^{N+2}\,\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{N+2}{2}+\gamma\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{N+2}{2}+\gamma}}\,e^{irx}}{\varepsilon^{N+1}\,\kappa^{N}\,\dfrac{\Gamma\left(\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}\,e^{irx}}\sim\varepsilon^{2}\,\kappa^{2}\,\left(\frac{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}{X_{0}-X}\right).

We want consecutive terms to become the same order as
N\to\infty
, so we need

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}\,\frac{\absolutevalue{\frac{N}{2} + \gamma}}{\absolutevalue{X_0 - X}}\sim 1,\qquad\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}which implies}}\qquad\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\sim\frac{\absolutevalue{X_0 - X}}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu,

where
\nu
is a small finite number used to ensure that
N
is an even natural number.

We seek to find the Stokes’ line, which is a singularity that triggers the dominant part of the remainder. With that in mind, we make the change
X-X_{0}=\rho\,e^{i\theta}
, where
\rho>0
and
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
, which implies that
\dfrac{N}{2}+\gamma\sim\dfrac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu
, and
X=X_{0}+\rho\,e^{i\theta}
. Therefore, when using the phase shifts introduced in (
8
), we have

\displaystyle e^{i\hat{x}}=e^{i\left(X-X_{0}+X_{0}\right)/\varepsilon^{2}-i\hat{\chi}}=e^{i\left(X-X_{0}\right)/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{iX_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}.

Furthermore, by using Stirling’s approximation, we note that

\displaystyle e^{i\left(X-X_{0}\right)/\varepsilon^{2}}\,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}

\displaystyle\sim e^{i\left(X-X_{0}\right)/\varepsilon^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\frac{1}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}\,\sqrt{\frac{2\,\pi}{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}\,\left(\frac{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}{e}\right)^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,e^{i\left(X-X_{0}\right)/\varepsilon^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\frac{1}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}\,\left(\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\right)^{\frac{N-1}{2}+\gamma}\,\frac{1}{e^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}

\displaystyle\sim\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\varepsilon^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\frac{1}{\left(-\rho\,e^{i\,\theta}\right)^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}\,\left(\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\right)^{\frac{N-1}{2}+\gamma}\,\frac{e^{i\rho\,e^{i\theta}/\varepsilon^{2}}}{e^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\varepsilon^{2\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)}\,\kappa^{2\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)}}{\left(\rho\,e^{i\,(\theta+\pi)}\right)^{\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu}}\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)^{\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu-\frac{1}{2}}\,\frac{e^{i\rho\,e^{i\theta}/\varepsilon^{2}}}{e^{\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu}}

\displaystyle\sim\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\frac{\kappa^{2\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)}}{\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)}}\,\frac{1}{\left(e^{i(\theta+\pi)}\right)^{\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu}}\,e^{\nu}\,\frac{\varepsilon\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,\frac{e^{i\rho\,e^{i\theta}/\varepsilon^{2}}}{e^{\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\varepsilon\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\frac{\kappa^{2\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)}}\,\frac{e^{-\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}}}{\left(e^{i(\theta+\pi)}\right)^{\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu}}\,e^{i\rho\,e^{i\theta}/\varepsilon^{2}}.

Here, observe that,
when
\theta=-\dfrac{\pi}{2}
, we have
e^{i\rho\,e^{i\theta}/\varepsilon^{2}}=e^{\rho/\varepsilon^{2}}
, which implies that
this quantity is exponentially small except in a neighborhood of the Stokes’ line,
\theta=-\dfrac{\pi}{2}
. Therefore, we let
\theta=-\dfrac{\pi}{2}+\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}
, which implies

\displaystyle e^{i\left(X-X_{0}\right)/\varepsilon^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}\sim\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\varepsilon\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\kappa^{2\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)}}\,\frac{e^{-\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}}}{\left(e^{i\left(\frac{\pi}{2}+\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}\right)}\right)^{\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu}}\,e^{\rho\,e^{i\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}}/\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\sim\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\varepsilon\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\frac{\kappa^{2\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)}}\,\frac{e^{-\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}}}{\left(i\,e^{i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}}\right)^{\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu}}\,e^{\rho\,\left(1+i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\,\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}\right)/\varepsilon^{2}}.

Now, if we take
\kappa^{2}=\kappa_{+}^{2}
, then

\displaystyle e^{i\left(X-X_{0}\right)/\varepsilon^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}

\displaystyle\sim\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\varepsilon\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\frac{1}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,\frac{e^{-\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}}}{\left(e^{i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}}\right)^{\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu}}\,e^{\rho\,\left(1+i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}\,\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}\right)/\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\varepsilon\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}

Furthermore, with the scales we have defined, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{r}

\displaystyle=h_{r}^{[0]}\,\left(\frac{1+2\,i\,\kappa^{2}\,r}{\kappa^{4}}-\varepsilon^{2}\,\left(\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\right)\,\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}\,\left(X-X_{0}\right)}\right)\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}

\displaystyle\sim h_{r}^{[0]}\,\left(\frac{1+2\,i\,\kappa^{2}\,r}{\kappa^{4}}-\varepsilon^{2}\,\left(\frac{\rho}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}+\nu\right)\,\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}\,\rho\,e^{i\,\left(-\frac{\pi}{2}+\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}\right)}}\right)\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}

\displaystyle\sim h_{r}^{[0]}\,\left(\frac{1+2\,i\,\kappa^{2}\,r}{\kappa^{4}}-\frac{i}{\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}\,\kappa^{2}\,e^{i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}}}\right)\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{h_{r}^{[0]}}{\kappa^{2}}\,\left(\frac{1+2\,i\,\kappa^{2}\,r}{\kappa^{2}}-\frac{i}{\absolutevalue{\kappa}^{2}}\,\left(1-i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}\right)\right)\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}

\displaystyle\sim h_{r}^{[0]}\,\left(2\,r-2+i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}\right)\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]},

which implies

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}_{0}\sim h_{0}^{[0]}\,\left(-2+i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}\right)\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbf{C}_{2}\sim h_{2}^{[0]}\,\left(2+i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}\right)\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}.

(76)

Therefore, the second line of (
75
) is, asymptotically, given by

\varepsilon^{N+1}\,\kappa^{N}\,e^{ix}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}\sum_{r=0,2}e^{i(r-1)\hat{x}}\,\mathbf{C}_{r}\\
=\varepsilon^{1-2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{iX_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\left(e^{i\left(X-X_{0}\right)/\varepsilon^{2}}\,\varepsilon^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{N+2\,\gamma}\,\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{N}{2}+\gamma\right)}{\left(X_{0}-X\right)^{\frac{N}{2}+\gamma}}\right)\,\sum_{r=0,2}e^{i(r-1)x}\,\mathbf{C}_{r}\\
\sim\varepsilon^{1-2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{iX_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\left(\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\varepsilon\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\right)\,\sum_{r=0,2}e^{i(r-1)\hat{x}}\,\mathbf{C}_{r}\\
\sim\varepsilon^{2-2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{iX_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,k^{2}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\\
\times\left(\left(-2+i\,\hat{\theta}\,\varepsilon\right)\,h_{0}^{[0]}\,e^{-i\hat{x}}+\left(2+i\,\hat{\theta}\,\varepsilon\right)\,h_{2}^{[0]}\,e^{i\hat{x}}\right)\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{\theta}}=\frac{\partial\theta}{\partial\hat{\theta}}\,\frac{\partial X}{\partial\theta}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial X}=i\,\rho\,e^{i\,\theta}\,\varepsilon\,\frac{\partial}{\partial X}=\rho\,e^{i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}}\,\varepsilon\,\frac{\partial}{\partial X},

which yields

\displaystyle X_{0}-X

\displaystyle=-\rho\,e^{i\theta}=i\,\rho\,e^{i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}},

\displaystyle e^{i\hat{x}}

\displaystyle=e^{\rho\,e^{i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{iX_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial X}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-i\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}}}{\rho\,\varepsilon}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{\theta}}.

Thus, (
75
) becomes

\mathbf{0}=\sum_{p=0}^{M_{a}}\,\sum_{q=0}^{M_{b}}a^{p}\,b^{q}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{p,q}(\mathbf{S})\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}+k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N,2}}{\partial x^{2}}+2\,\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho}\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N,2}}{\partial x\partial\hat{\theta}}e^{-i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N,2}}{\partial\hat{\theta}^{2}}e^{-2\,i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}}\right)\\
+\varepsilon^{2-2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{iX_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,k^{2}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\\
\times\left(\left(-2+i\,\hat{\theta}\,\varepsilon\right)\,h_{0}^{[0]}\,e^{-ix}+\left(2+i\,\hat{\theta}\,\varepsilon\right)\,h_{2}^{[0]}\,e^{ix}\right)\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]},

which can be expanded as

\mathbf{0}=\sum_{p=0}^{M_{a}}\,\sum_{q=0}^{M_{b}}a^{p}\,b^{q}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{p,q}(\mathbf{S})\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}\\
+k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N,2}}{\partial x^{2}}+2\,\frac{\varepsilon}{\rho}\left(1-i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}\right)\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N,2}}{\partial x\partial\hat{\theta}}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\,\left(1-2\,i\,\varepsilon\,\hat{\theta}\right)\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N,2}}{\partial\hat{\theta}^{2}}\right)\\
+\varepsilon^{2-2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{iX_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,k^{2}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\\
\times\left(\left(-2+i\,\hat{\theta}\,\varepsilon\right)\,h_{0}^{[0]}\,e^{-ix}+\left(2+i\,\hat{\theta}\,\varepsilon\right)\,h_{2}^{[0]}\,e^{ix}\right)\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]},\hskip 14.22636pt

(77)

Now, we proceed to solve (
77
). To do so, we use the following expansion:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{N,2}=\varepsilon^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{iX_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,\sum_{j\geq 1}\varepsilon^{j}\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[j]}.

Therefore, when solving this equation order by order, we have

Order
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)
:

At this order, (
77
) becomes

\displaystyle\left(J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,0}(\mathbf{0})+k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}\right)\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}=\mathbf{0},

which has the following solution:

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}

\displaystyle=\left(C_{1}\left(\hat{\theta}\right)\,e^{ix}+C_{-1}\left(\hat{\theta}\right)\,e^{-ix}\right)\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]},

and we will omit the dependence on
\hat{\theta}
from now on when there is no confusion.

Order
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{2}\right)
:

At this order, (
77
) becomes

\displaystyle\left(J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,0}(\mathbf{0})+k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[2]}

\displaystyle=-J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,0,0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}-a_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{1,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}

\displaystyle\quad-b_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,1}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}-\frac{2}{\rho}\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}}{\partial x\partial\hat{\theta}}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\,\left(h_{0}^{[0]}\,e^{-ix}\,\left(2\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\,\left(h_{2}^{[0]}\,e^{ix}\,\left(2\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}\right)\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[2]}=2\,\left(A_{1}\,C_{-1}+\bar{A}_{1}\,C_{1}\right)\,\mathbf{W}_{0}^{[2]}+\left(C_{1}\,e^{ix}+C_{-1}\,e^{-ix}\right)\,\mathbf{W}_{1}^{[2]}-\frac{1}{\rho}\,\left(i\,C_{1}^{\prime}\,e^{ix}-i\,C_{-1}^{\prime}\,e^{-ix}\right)\mathbf{W}_{1,3}^{[3]}

\displaystyle-\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\,h_{0}^{[0]}\,e^{-ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{1,3}^{[3]}+\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\,h_{2}^{[0]}\,e^{ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{1,3}^{[3]}

\displaystyle+2\,\left(A_{1}\,C_{1}\,e^{2ix}+\bar{A}_{1}\,C_{-1}\,e^{-2ix}\right)\,\mathbf{W}_{2}^{[2]}.

Order
\mathcal{O}\left(\varepsilon^{3}\right)
:

At this order, (
77
) becomes

\displaystyle\left(J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,0}(\mathbf{0})+k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{2}}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[3]}

\displaystyle=-J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,0,0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[2]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}-a_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,1,0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}

\displaystyle-b_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,0,1}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}-J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{2,0,0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]},\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}

\displaystyle-J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{F}_{1,0,0}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[2]}-a_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{1,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[2]}-b_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,1}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[2]}

\displaystyle-a_{2}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{1,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}-b_{2}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,1}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}-a_{1}^{2}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{2,0}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}

\displaystyle-a_{1}\,b_{1}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{1,1}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}-b_{1}^{2}\,J_{\mathbf{u}}\,\mathbf{f}_{0,2}(\mathbf{0})\,\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}-\frac{2}{\rho}\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[2]}}{\partial x\partial\hat{\theta}}

\displaystyle+2\,i\,\frac{\hat{\theta}}{\rho}\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}}{\partial x\partial\hat{\theta}}-\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\,k^{2}\,\hat{D}\,\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbf{R}_{N,2}^{[1]}}{\partial\hat{\theta}^{2}}

\displaystyle-i\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\frac{\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,k^{2}}{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\,\hat{\theta}\,\left(h_{0}^{[0]}\,e^{-ix}+h_{2}^{[0]}\,\,e^{ix}\right)\,\hat{D}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}.

Now, as usual, we need to determine solvability conditions to ensure that this equation has a solution. In particular, we obtain two solvability conditions given by

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{D}\,\mathbf{W}_{1,3}^{[3]},\bm{\psi}\right\rangle\,\frac{2\,k^{2}\,\left(C_{-1}^{\prime\prime}-i\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\rho^{3/2}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\,\hat{\theta}\,h_{0}^{[0]}\right)}{\rho^{2}}

\displaystyle=0,

(78)

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{D}\,\mathbf{W}_{1,3}^{[3]},\bm{\psi}\right\rangle\,\frac{2\,k^{2}\,\left(C_{1}^{\prime\prime}-i\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\rho^{3/2}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\,\hat{\theta}\,h_{2}^{[0]}\right)}{\rho^{2}}

\displaystyle=0,

(79)

where
′
denotes the derivative with respect to
\theta
, and we have assumed that
a_{1}+b_{1}=0
to ensure the convergence of the expressions we develop below, noting that, generically,
a_{1}=b_{1}=0
(see (
107
)). This yields

\displaystyle C_{-1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=i\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\rho^{3/2}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\,\hat{\theta}\,h_{0}^{[0]},

\displaystyle C_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=i\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\rho^{3/2}\,e^{-\rho\,\frac{\hat{\theta}^{2}}{2}}\,\hat{\theta}\,h_{2}^{[0]}.

Now, when integrating these expressions, we note that

\displaystyle C_{-1}

\displaystyle=-i\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\,h_{0}^{[0]}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\hat{\theta}}e^{-\frac{\nu^{2}\,\rho}{2}}\differential\nu,

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle=-i\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\sqrt{2\,\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\,h_{2}^{[0]}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\hat{\theta}}e^{-\frac{\nu^{2}\,\rho}{2}}\,\differential\nu,

which implies

\displaystyle C_{-1}

\displaystyle=-2\,i\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\sqrt{\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,h_{0}^{[0]}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\sqrt{\frac{\rho}{2}}\,\hat{\theta}}e^{-\nu^{2}}\,\differential\nu,

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle=-2\,i\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\sqrt{\pi}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,h_{2}^{[0]}\,\int_{-\infty}^{\sqrt{\frac{\rho}{2}}\,\hat{\theta}}e^{-\nu^{2}}\,\differential\nu.

where we have set the condition
C_{-1},C_{1}\to 0
as
\hat{\theta}\to-\infty
. Thus,
when
we cross the Stokes’ line as
\hat{\theta}
goes from
-\infty
to
\infty
,

\displaystyle-2\,i\,\pi\,\varepsilon^{-2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{iX_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\left(h_{2}^{[0]}\,e^{ix}+h_{0}^{[0]}\,e^{-ix}\right)\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}

(80)

gets triggered by
\kappa_{+}^{2}
. Furthermore, by symmetry, we have that

\displaystyle 2\,i\,\pi\,\varepsilon^{-2\,\bar{\gamma}}\,\kappa^{-2\,\bar{\gamma}}\,e^{-i\bar{X}_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{i\hat{\chi}}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\left(\overline{h_{2}^{[0]}}\,e^{-ix}+\overline{h_{0}^{[0]}}\,e^{ix}\right)\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}

(81)

gets triggered by
\kappa_{-}^{2}
.

Now, let us recall that the outer solution we had obtained for the remainder in Section
5
is given by
\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[1]}=B_{1}\,e^{ix}\,\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}+c.c.,
where
B_{1}
is given by (
5
). Now, as in
[
10
]
, we note that
L_{1}
(respectively,
L_{4}
) corresponds to translating the solution
\mathbf{u}^{[1]}
in
X
(respectively,
x
). Therefore, these are not relevant to our analysis. The constant we need to focus on is
L_{2}
, which controls the solution triggered at the Stokes’ line. Therefore, for simplicity, we take
L_{1}=L_{3}=L_{4}=0
, which yields

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{multlined}\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\,\left(\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{L_{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s-\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{\delta\left(R_{1}\right)}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s\right)\right.\\
\left.-i\,\frac{\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}}{2\,\alpha_{1}}\,R_{1}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}R_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\left(\int_{X_{0}}^{\omega}\frac{L_{2}}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s-\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{\omega}\frac{\delta\left(R_{1}\right)}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\,\differential s\right)\,\differential\omega\right)\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}(X)}.\end{multlined}

Furthermore, if we focus on the contribution of the term related to
L_{2}
in order to match solutions, we have that

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle=L_{2}\,\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\frac{\differential s}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}-i\,\frac{\alpha_{3}+\alpha_{4}}{2\,\alpha_{1}}\,R_{1}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{X}\left(R_{1}\,R_{1}^{\prime}\,\int_{X_{0}}^{\omega}\frac{\differential s}{\left(R_{1}^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right)\,\differential\omega\right)\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}(X)},

(82)

\displaystyle=\frac{L_{2}}{K_{2}}\,h_{2}^{[0]},

which has an asymptotic behavior given by,

\displaystyle B_{1}\sim-L_{2}\,\frac{\sqrt{2}\,\beta_{3}}{8\,\beta_{1}^{2}}\,\sqrt{-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{3}}}\,(1+2\,\eta\,i)\,e^{2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,X}\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}(X)},

(83)

as
X\to\infty
.

Moreover, when matching (
82
) and (
80
) with
\kappa^{2}=\kappa_{+}^{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle L_{2}=L_{2}^{+}

\displaystyle=-2\,i\,\pi\,\varepsilon^{-2\,\gamma}\,\kappa^{-2\,\gamma}\,e^{iX_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{-i\hat{\chi}}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,K_{2}.

(84)

On the other hand, when considering
\kappa^{2}=\kappa_{-}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle L_{2}=L_{2}^{-}

\displaystyle=2\,i\,\pi\,\varepsilon^{-2\,\bar{\gamma}}\,\kappa^{-2\,\bar{\gamma}}\,e^{-i\bar{X}_{0}/\varepsilon^{2}}\,e^{i\hat{\chi}}\,\absolutevalue{\kappa}\,\bar{K}_{2}.

(85)

Remark 6
.

We highlight a key difference between our formulation in equation (
75
) and
[
10
, Equation 120]
and
[
13
, Equation 7.4]
. Although the approach followed here follows the same ideas as those developed in
[
10
,
13
]
, we have to bear in mind that the Swift-Hohenberg equation is a fourth-order partial differential equation, which implies that the definition of
X=\varepsilon^{2}\,x
produces several terms out of
\partial_{xxxx}u
. Therefore, the authors of those papers simplified the equation of the remainder by using the explicit form of their model, whilst making an abuse of notation by using terms that are not part of the expansion to simplify expressions. In our context, as a stationary reaction-diffusion equation is only a second-order partial differential equation, then that is not necessary, as the forcing due to truncation gets reduced to the last three terms in (
75
).

6.1
Final prediction of the width of homoclinic snaking

Merging all the information we have developed so far, we summarize the expressions that need to be used to determine the width of the homoclinic snaking close to codimension-two Turing bifurcation points
to conclude with the bound given by (
2
)
.

In particular, from (
83
), we have that the homogeneous part of the amplitude of the remainder, considering the contribution from
\kappa_{+}^{2}
and
\kappa_{-}^{2}
, is given by:

\displaystyle B_{1,h}\sim\left(-L_{2}^{+}\,\frac{\sqrt{2}\,\beta_{3}}{8\,\beta_{1}^{2}}-L_{2}^{-}\,\frac{\sqrt{2}\,\beta_{3}}{8\,\beta_{1}^{2}}\right)\,\sqrt{-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{3}}}\,(1+2\,\eta\,i)\,e^{2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,X}\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}(X)},

as
X\to\infty
. On the other hand, the term associated with the separation from the Maxwell point introduced in equation (
66
) will produce a particular solution with the form

\displaystyle B_{1,p}\sim R(\varepsilon)\,\delta b\,\sqrt{-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{3}}}\,(1+2\,\eta\,i)\,e^{2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,X}\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}(X)},

as
X\to\infty
, where
R(\varepsilon)
is a rational function in
\varepsilon
that needs to be determined for each system one wants to study (see examples in Section
7
). Thus, by considering these two contributions, we have that
B_{1}
has an asymptotic behaviour given by

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle\sim\left(-\frac{\sqrt{2}\,\beta_{3}}{4\,\beta_{1}^{2}}\,\real\left(L_{2}^{+}\right)+p(\delta b,\varepsilon)\right)\,\sqrt{-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{3}}}\,(1+2\,\eta\,i)\,e^{2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,X}\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}(X)}

(86)

\displaystyle=\begin{multlined}\left(-\frac{\sqrt{2}\,\beta_{3}}{2\,\beta_{1}^{2}}\,\frac{\pi\,\absolutevalue{K_2}\,e^{-\frac{\pi}{2}\,\left(\frac{1}{k\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,\varepsilon^{2}}+\eta\right)}}{\varepsilon^{6}}\,\cos\left(K_{2}^{o}-\hat{\chi}+2\,\eta\,\log(\varepsilon)\right)+R(\varepsilon)\,\delta b\right)\\
\times\sqrt{-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{3}}}\,(1+2\,\eta\,i)\,e^{2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,X}\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}(X)},\end{multlined}

as
X\to\infty
, where
K_{2}=\absolutevalue{K_2}\,e^{i\,K_{2}^{o}}
. Therefore, in order to have a valid expansion, we need to ensure that this expression tends to zero as
X\to\infty
, so we need the coefficient of
e^{2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,X}
to equal 0. Thus, as the cosine function is bounded between
-1
and
1
, we have that there exists a value of
\hat{\chi}
so that said coefficient equals zero if and only if

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\delta b}\leq\absolutevalue{\frac{\sqrt{2} \, \beta_3}{2 \, \beta_1^2 \, R(\varepsilon)}}\,\frac{\pi\,\absolutevalue{K_2}\,e^{-\frac{\pi}{2}\,\left(\frac{1}{k\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,\varepsilon^{2}}+\eta\right)}}{\varepsilon^{6}},

(87)

where the coefficients
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{3}
are defined in (
21
) and
K_{2}
, defined in (
61
), requires the determination of
\lambda_{1}
, which is a generic expression for the terms
c_{r}^{[0]}
defined by the limit (
43
). We highlight that (
87
) provides an exponentially decaying bound for the width of the snaking as
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
.

We highlight that although this completes the main purpose of this article by giving us the width of the homoclinic snaking, an extra step is required to do a complete analysis of the snaking. That step involves joining the two fronts that we have been studying (see sub-Section
2.3
) to obtain an approximation of the bifurcation curves close to the codimension-two Turing bifurcation point. We make that analysis for completeness, but we relegate it to
C
.

7
Examples

We now proceed to illustrate the theory by computing the necessary components of the beyond-all-orders theory for several common examples. Readers are invited to run the code for each example themselves, which is provided at
[
24
]
, using Python and Mathematica (to understand the logic of the code, the reader is invited to read
F
). In each case, we compare our analytical width of the snake given by (
87
) with a numerical evaluation of fold points of homoclinic trajectories of the associated spatial-dynamics problem on the real line, using AUTO
[
14
]
.

7.1
Swift-Hohenberg 2-3

We start by applying our general theory to the Swift-Hohenberg equation, which was first deduced from the equations for thermal convection. That equation, with quadratic and cubic nonlinearities, was studied in
[
10
]
, and served as the main inspiration for this paper. We highlight that said equation has been studied from different points of view and different nonlinearities as it is one of the simplest pattern-forming equations (see, e.g.
[
6
,
7
,
13
,
21
]
, and references therein). The Swift-Hohenberg 2-3 equation can be written as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u=-C\,u-3\,E\,u^{2}-u^{3}-\left(1+\partial_{xx}\right)^{2}u,

where
u=u(x,t)
is a real variable. Nevertheless, as explained in Section
1
, this type of equation can be written as (
1
) simply by defining an auxiliary variable
v=\partial_{xx}u
as:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u

\displaystyle=-(1+C)\,u-3\,E\,u^{2}-u^{3}-2\,v-\partial_{xx}v,

(88)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=v-\partial_{xx}u,

and we use
a=C
, and
b=E
.

To evidence the use of the code shared to study this type of problem, we state the parameter expansions as they were considered in
[
10
]
:

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=C_{4}\,\varepsilon^{4},

(89)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=E_{0}+E_{2}\,\varepsilon^{2}+E_{4}\,\varepsilon^{4}+\delta E.

Now, we note that we can obtain the values of the variables shown in Table
1
simply by running the Python code at
[
24
]
. In particular, we highlight that the values obtained are consistent with the results obtained in
[
10
]
.

Variable

C_{4}

E_{0}

E_{2}

E_{4}

\alpha_{1}

\alpha_{3}

\alpha_{5}

\alpha_{6}

\alpha_{7}

Value

1

\dfrac{\sqrt{114}}{38}

4\,\dfrac{\sqrt{20919}}{1083}

\dfrac{63711\,\sqrt{114}}{10069012}

4

\dfrac{16}{19}

- 1

8\,\dfrac{\sqrt{734}}{19}

-\dfrac{8820}{361}

Table 1
:
Values of the key parameters in the asymptotic expansion of system (
88
).
The values of the parameters that are not shown, but have the same name format with a different subscript to the ones presented equal zero. These variables were obtained with the Python code in
[
24
]
, with the variable
modelname=‘Swift-Hohenberg’
.

To find the width of the snaking, we need to focus on the fifth-order equation for the remainder, (
66
). To study that equation, we note that the only terms that will affect the remainder are given by the linearization of (
88
) at
(u,v)=(0,0)
, and we add
-3\,\delta E\,u^{2}
to the equation for
u
to take into account the separation of
E
from the Maxwell point. In particular, we need such a term to appear in the solvability condition at order five. With this in mind, we note that
\mathbf{F}_{2,0,1}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]},\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)
will not have resonant terms at that order. The first of those resonant terms will turn up due to
2\,\mathbf{F}_{2,0,1}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]},\mathbf{u}^{[2]}\right)
at order five, which lets us know that the first term coming from the quadratic expression needs to be part of
\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[4]}
for the resonant term to appear at order five. In particular, when using the same notation as before, we have that
\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[4]}
is given by (
64
) plus

\displaystyle\frac{\delta E}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,\mathbf{W}_{*}^{[4]}+\frac{\delta E}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,A_{1}^{2}\,e^{2ix}\,\mathbf{W}_{*,2}^{[4]}+c.c.,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{0}\,\mathbf{W}_{*}^{[4]}

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{F}_{2,0,1}\left(\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]},\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{2}\,\mathbf{W}_{*,2}^{[4]}

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{F}_{2,0,1}\left(\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]},\bm{\phi}_{1}^{[1]}\right),

and the terms
\varepsilon^{2}
in the denominators are put to match the orders of
\mathbf{R}_{N}^{[4]}
and
\mathbf{F}_{2,0,1}\left(\mathbf{u}^{[1]},\mathbf{u}^{[1]}\right)
.

With this, we conclude that the equation for the amplitude of the remainder, (
66
), becomes

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}\,B_{1}^{\prime\prime}+i\,\alpha_{3}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,B_{1}^{\prime}+i\,\alpha_{3}\,A_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}\,\bar{B}_{1}+i\,\alpha_{3}\,\bar{A}_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}\,B_{1}+\alpha_{5}\,B_{1}+\alpha_{6}\,A_{1}^{2}\,\bar{B}_{1}+2\,\alpha_{6}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,B_{1}

\displaystyle+2\,\alpha_{7}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,A_{1}^{2}\,\bar{B}_{1}+3\,\alpha_{7}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{4}\,B_{1}+2\,\sqrt{114}\,\frac{\delta E}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,A_{1}=0,

which implies that
\dfrac{\differential\delta}{\differential A_{1}}\left(A_{1}\right)=2\,\sqrt{114}\,\delta E\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,A_{1}
. Therefore, (see (
86
))

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle\sim\sqrt{-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{3}}}\,\left(-\frac{\sqrt{2}\,\beta_{3}\,L_{2}^{+}}{8\,\beta_{1}^{2}}-\frac{\sqrt{2}\,\beta_{3}\,L_{2}^{-}}{8\,\beta_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{57}\,\delta E}{2\,\varepsilon^{2}\,\alpha_{1}\,\beta_{3}}\right)\,(1+2\,\eta\,i)\,e^{X}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\,\sqrt{-\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{3}}}\,(1+2\,\eta\,i)\,e^{X}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(-\frac{\sqrt{2}\,\beta_{3}}{\beta_{1}^{2}}\,\frac{\pi\,\absolutevalue{K_2}\,e^{-\frac{\pi}{2}\,\left(\eta+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}}{\varepsilon^{6}}\,\cos(K_2^o - \hat\chi+ 2 \, \eta\, \log(\varepsilon))+\frac{\sqrt{57}\,\delta E}{\varepsilon^{2}\,\alpha_{1}\,\beta_{3}}\right),

as
X\to\infty
, where
\beta_{1},\beta_{3}
are defined in (
21
) and
K_{2}=\absolutevalue{K_2}\,e^{i\,K_{2}^{o}}
was defined in (
61
).

Thus, to ensure that our solution remains bounded, we need to force the coefficient of this expression to be zero, which implies that we need

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\delta E}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\,\alpha_{1}\,\beta_{3}^{2}}{\sqrt{57}\,\beta_{1}^{2}}\,\frac{\pi\,\absolutevalue{K_2}\,e^{-\frac{\pi}{2}\,\left(\eta+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}}{\varepsilon^{4}},

(90)

for there to exist a value of
\hat{\chi}
so that
B_{1}
decays to zero as
X
tends to infinity. Note that (
90
) is an exponentially small term as
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
.

Now, note that all the variables in (
90
) are known except for
K_{2}
. To determine that variable, we need to run the recurrence given by (
35
) with the initial conditions given by

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{19}\,\sqrt[4]{98861726}\,i}{734},

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle=\begin{multlined}\frac{\sqrt{19}\,\sqrt[4]{734}}{360647576448}\,\left(\left(-345333190+2602107\,\pi\,i\right)\,\sqrt{367}\right.\\
\left.+\left(954973269\,\pi+236726744\,i\right)\,\sqrt{2}\right),\end{multlined}

which correspond to the leading order coefficients of
A_{1}
and
A_{3}
, respectively, at
X_{0}
(see ansatz (
34
)). This is done to obtain an approximation of
c_{2}^{[0]}
, given by (
43
). In particular, when running
n
up to 181, we obtain the graphs shown in Figure
4
, where panel (a) (respectively, (b)) shows the convergence of the magnitude (respectively, angle) of
c_{2}^{[0]}
. In particular, the dotted lines correspond to the approximation of each of these quantities for different values of
n/2
, and the continuous line is a graph of the best curve of the form

\displaystyle f(n)=c_{0}+\frac{c_{1}}{n}+\frac{c_{2}}{n^{2}}

(91)

that fits each set of points, ignoring the first few points. These curves were obtained using the procedure outlined in
[
13
]
, and the lines appear to approximate the curves well. In particular, this lets us know that
c_{2}^{[0]}\to 0.0303085\,e^{-2.57629\,i}
as
n\to\infty
, which implies that (see (
61
)):

\displaystyle K_{2}=-\frac{6\,i\,\left(-2\,\beta_{3}\right)^{-1/2}}{(3-2\,\eta\,i)}\,c_{0}^{[0]}\approx 0.0300695+0.0195978\,i,

and

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{K_2}=0.0358922,\qquad K_{2}^{o}=0.577605.

With this, we can see that the width of the snaking in terms of
\varepsilon
is approximately given by

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\delta E}\leq\frac{2.82934\,e^{-\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon^{2}}}}{\varepsilon^{4}},

(92)

which is close to the function obtained by numerical fitting in
[
10
, Equation (163)]
. A numerical graph showing the fit of this function compared to numerics is shown in Figure
5
, which shows that our approximation of the width of the homoclinic snaking is remarkably good.

). (a) Behaviour of the absolute value of the argument of the limit in (

.

in

.

.

7.2
Swift-Hohenberg 3-5

As a second example, we study the same type of equation we studied previously, but with different nonlinearities. In particular, we now apply our theory to the Swift-Hohenberg 3-5 equation, which can be written as

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u

\displaystyle=r\,u-u-2\,v+s\,u^{3}-u^{5}-v_{xx},

(93)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=v-\partial_{xx}u,

and we use
a=s
and
b=r
. This example is motivated by the fact that it has the symmetry
(u,v)\to-(u,v)
, which changes the dominant value of
\kappa
and the dominant modes to be taken into account to study the width of the snaking
[
13
]
. In particular, we show that the theory we have developed can also be applied in these cases, as shown in
B
.

First, we note that a degenerate Turing bifurcation in (
93
) occurs at
(r,s)=(0,0)
with a wavenumber
k=1
. Furthermore, the parameters obtained when making the expansions as developed throughout this article are shown in Table
2
.

Variable

r_{4}

s_{2}

\alpha_{1}

\alpha_{5}

\alpha_{6}

\alpha_{7}

\beta_{1}

\beta_{3}

\beta_{5}

Value

-\dfrac{27}{160}

1

4

-\dfrac{27}{160}

3

-10

\dfrac{27}{640}

-\dfrac{3}{8}

\dfrac{5}{6}

Table 2
:
Values of some of the key parameters in the asymptotic expansion of system (
93
).
The values of the parameters that are not shown, but have the same name format with a different subscript to the ones presented equal zero. These variables were obtained with the Python code in
[
24
]
, with the variable
modelname=‘SHDM’
.

As in the previous example, to obtain an approximation of the width of the snaking, it is important to know
c_{0}^{[0]}
and
c_{2}^{[0]}
. Nevertheless, due to the special symmetry
(u,v)\to-(u,v)
(
93
) has, these terms equal zero. The dominant modes in this case are
r=\pm 1,\pm 3
, so we need to compute
c_{1}^{[0]}
and
c_{3}^{[0]}
(see sub-Section
3.2
). The convergence of
c_{1}^{[0]}
when using our code with initial conditions

\displaystyle A_{1}=\frac{10^{\frac{3}{4}}\,\sqrt[4]{3}\,i}{10},\qquad\text{and}\qquad A_{3}=-\frac{10^{\frac{3}{4}}\,\sqrt[4]{3}}{16}

is shown in Figure
6
. The convergence for
c_{3}^{[0]}
looks very similar to the one for
c_{1}^{[0]}
and is, therefore, omitted (the result can be checked by using the code that is contained in the GitHub repository provided in
[
24
]
). We highlight that the results obtained are consistent with
[
13
]
. In fact, we also conclude that
c_{1}^{[0]}\approx 0.101\,i
, which is the same value as was found in
[
13
]
, but with a different sign. The change in sign here is consistent with the choice of
\kappa^{2}=\kappa_{+}^{2}/2
to make our analysis (see
B
for an explanation of the division by 2).

Next, to complete this example, we note that the calculation in this case can be carried out using the same ansatzes at each stage, but with a few minor changes (see
B
). Now, note that the parameter
r
only influences the linear term in (
93
). Therefore, when considering the equation for the remainder, we only need to add a linear term to (
66
):

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}\,B_{1}^{\prime\prime}+i\,\alpha_{2}\,B_{1}^{\prime}+i\,\alpha_{3}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,B_{1}^{\prime}+i\,\alpha_{3}\,A_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}\,\bar{B}_{1}+i\,\alpha_{3}\,\bar{A}_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}\,B_{1}+i\,\alpha_{4}\,A_{1}^{2}\,\bar{B}_{1}^{\prime}+2\,i\,\alpha_{4}\,A_{1}\,\bar{A}_{1}^{\prime}\,B_{1}

\displaystyle+\alpha_{5}\,B_{1}+\alpha_{6}\,A_{1}^{2}\,\bar{B}_{1}+2\,\alpha_{6}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,B_{1}+2\,\alpha_{7}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,A_{1}^{2}\,\bar{B}_{1}+3\,\alpha_{7}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{4}\,B_{1}+\frac{\delta r}{\varepsilon^{4}}\,A_{1}=0,

which implies that
\dfrac{\differential\delta}{\differential A_{1}}\left(A_{1}\right)=\dfrac{\delta r}{\varepsilon^{4}}\,A_{1}
. Therefore, when taking into account the little changes one needs to make in order to study the width of the snaking for (
93
) (see
B
), we have

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle\sim\frac{1}{8\,\beta_{1}}\,\sqrt{-\frac{2\,\beta_{1}}{\beta_{3}}}\,\left(-L_{2}^{+}\,\frac{\beta_{3}}{\beta_{1}}-L_{2}^{-}\,\frac{\beta_{3}}{\beta_{1}}-\frac{\delta r}{\alpha_{1}}\right)\,e^{2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,X}\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8\,\beta_{1}}\,\sqrt{-\frac{2\,\beta_{1}}{\beta_{3}}}\,\left(-\frac{\beta_{3}}{\beta_{1}}\,\frac{16\,\pi\,\absolutevalue{K_2}\,e^{-\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,\varepsilon^{2}}}}{\varepsilon^{6}}\,\cos\left(K_{2}^{o}-2\,\hat{\chi}\right)-\frac{\delta r}{\varepsilon^{4}\,\alpha_{1}}\right)\,e^{2\,\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,X}\,e^{i\,\varphi_{1}},

as
X\to\infty
. With this, similar to the previous example, we need the condition

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\delta r}\leq-\frac{\alpha_{1}\,\beta_{3}}{\beta_{1}}\,\frac{16\,\pi\,\absolutevalue{K_2}\,e^{-\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{\beta_{1}}\,\varepsilon^{2}}}}{\varepsilon^{2}},

to ensure that there is a
value of
\hat{\chi}
to ensure that the remainder tends to 0 as
X\to\infty
, where
(see (
61
))

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{K_2}\approx 0.0202\,10^{\frac{3}{4}}\,\sqrt[4]{3}.

(a)

(b)

Figure 6
:
Similar to Figure
4
, but to approximate
c_{1}^{[0]}
for model (
93
).
Both of these graphs were obtained with the Mathematica code within the folders
Iteration/Swift-Hohenberg 3-5
in
[
24
]
.

Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\delta r}\leq\frac{267.183114\,e^{-4.868645\,\frac{\pi}{\varepsilon^{2}}}}{\varepsilon^{2}}.

Finally, when making the numerical check with actual snaking curves, we obtain the graph shown in Figure
7
, where we can see that the matching is, once again, excellent.

). The snaking in panel (b) (respectively, the solution in panel (c)) corresponds to
\varepsilon\approx 0.805579
(respectively,
r\approx-0.071071309973
).

7.3
Modified Schnakenberg system

To finish the study of models that have been studied in the past, we now deal with a modified version of the Schnakenberg system, which turns out to be a simple model for glycolysis that has been widely used to study pattern formation (see, e.g.
[
1
,
12
,
23
]
). The modified version of the Schnakenberg system we are to study here is given by:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u

\displaystyle=-u+u^{2}\,v+\sigma\,\left(u-\frac{1}{v}\right)^{2}+\partial_{xx}u,

(94)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v

\displaystyle=\lambda-u^{2}\,v-\sigma\,\left(u-\frac{1}{v}\right)^{2}+d\,\partial_{xx}v,

and is motivated by the study carried out in
[
12
]
. (
94
) has a homogeneous steady state given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}=(u,v)=\left(\lambda,\frac{1}{\lambda}\right).

Furthermore, if we fix
d=3+\sqrt{8}
, then this steady state goes through a codimension-two Turing bifurcation at

\displaystyle(\sigma,\lambda)=\left(\frac{21}{22}\,\sqrt{2}\,\sqrt{9407-6651\,\sqrt{2}}-\frac{15\,\sqrt{2}}{22}+\frac{15}{11}\,\sqrt{9407-6651\,\sqrt{2}}-\frac{31}{11},1\right).

Now, let us take
a=\lambda
and
b=\sigma
, and consider the translation
(u,v)\to(u,v)+\mathbf{P}
. With this, system (
94
) becomes

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u

\displaystyle=\left(\lambda\,u\,v+u+\lambda^{2}\,v\right)\,\left(\frac{u}{\lambda}+\frac{\sigma\,\left(\lambda\,u\,v+u+\lambda^{2}\,v\right)}{(\lambda\,v+1)^{2}}+1\right)+\partial_{xx}u,

(95)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v

\displaystyle=-\frac{u^{2}\,(\lambda\,v+1)+2\,\lambda\,u\,(\lambda\,v+1)+\lambda^{3}\,v}{\lambda}-\frac{\sigma\left(\lambda\,u\,v+u+\lambda^{2}\,v\right)^{2}}{(\lambda\,v+1)^{2}}+d\,\partial_{xx}v.

Now, we note that, when getting away from the Maxwell point with
\sigma
, that parameter influences infinitely many degrees of nonlinearities. Nevertheless, we only need to consider the dominant ones, which, in this case, are the quadratic and cubic ones. In particular, following the same idea as in Example
7.1
, we have that the equation for the amplitude of the remainder, (
66
), becomes

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}\,B_{1}^{\prime\prime}+i\,\alpha_{3}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,B_{1}^{\prime}+i\,\alpha_{3}\,A_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}\,\bar{B}_{1}+i\,\alpha_{3}\,\bar{A}_{1}\,A_{1}^{\prime}\,B_{1}+\alpha_{5}\,B_{1}+\alpha_{6}\,A_{1}^{2}\,\bar{B}_{1}+2\,\alpha_{6}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,B_{1}

\displaystyle+2\,\alpha_{7}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,A_{1}^{2}\,\bar{B}_{1}+3\,\alpha_{7}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{4}\,B_{1}-\frac{2}{3}\,\sqrt{9407-6651\,\sqrt{2}}\,\frac{\delta\,\sigma}{\varepsilon^{2}}\,\absolutevalue{A_1}^{2}\,A_{1}=0.

Furthermore, in this case, after running the
Mathematica code given in
[
24
]
to carry out the recurrence
, (
35
), with initial conditions

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle=0.833888471879154\,i,

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-0.544581934387067-0.383829250137414\,i},

we obtain that
c_{2}^{[0]}\approx{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0.126613}\,e^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-0.39059}\,i}
, which implies that
\absolutevalue{K_2}\approx{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0.210505288072226}

(see (
61
))
. The graph showing the convergence of
c_{2}^{[0]}
is shown in Figure
8
.

for model (
